Logged rather than thrown so a data typo // surfaces during development without crashing the app. BLOCKS.forEach((b) => { const used = Object.values(b.smdSeatsByParty).reduce((a, c) => a + c, 0); if (used !== b.smdSeats && typeof console !== "undefined") { console.warn(`Block ${b.id}: smdSeatsByParty sums to ${used}, expected ${b.smdSeats}`); } }); // ---- D'Hondt allocation (exact method used for Japan's PR tier) ---- // `caps` (optional) models a real quirk of the 2026 election: in several // blocks the LDP won more PR seats by vote share than it had list // candidates for (most were dual candidates who'd already won their // constituency race), so it forfeited 14 seats nationally to the next // party in the D'Hondt sequence. Wikipedia confirms this by name for // Tokyo (-5), Southern Kantō (-6), Hokuriku-Shin'etsu (-2) and Chūgoku // (-1). A capacity cap here reproduces that cascade under any swing. function dHondt(votePct, seats, caps) { const quotients = []; PARTY_IDS.forEach((p) => { if (PR_INELIGIBLE.has(p)) return; const v = votePct[p] || 0; if (v <= 0) return; for (let d = 1; d <= seats; d++) quotients.push({ p, q: v / d }); }); quotients.sort((a, b) => b.q - a.q); const alloc = {}; PARTY_IDS.forEach((p) => (alloc[p] = 0)); let filled = 0; for (let i = 0; i < quotients.length && filled < seats; i++) { const p = quotients[i].p; if (caps && caps[p] !== undefined && alloc[p] >= caps[p]) continue; alloc[p] += 1; filled += 1; } return alloc; } // Real geographic boundary data for all 289 constituencies (2022 // redistricting — matches the real 2026 election), sourced from a // shapefile (Ministry of Internal Affairs and Communications data via // 地域・交通データ研究所 / Akira Nishizawa, U-Tokyo CSIS), dissolved from // ~120,000 boundary fragments into 289 district polygons, simplified for // browser rendering, and pre-projected to SVG path data (viewBox 0 0 600 // 900). swing model: genuine universal national swing ---- // For each block, generate one notional seat per real winner (using the // directly-tabulated 2026 winner list in BLOCKS above — every seat's // baseline winner is real, not estimated). Each notional seat gets a // baseline vote-share profile: the assigned winner's share is set above // the block's strongest other party ("the generic challenger") by a // margin, with margins spread across a party's seats from marginal to // safe so the average roughly matches the block's own aggregate gap. // Applying swing then means literally what "universal national swing" // means: add the same national delta to every party's share in every // seat, and whoever has the highest post-swing share wins that seat. // This replaces an earlier "cube-law" curve-fit approximation, which // could produce disproportionate seat swings from modest vote changes — // see prior methodology notes. Real per-seat vote margins for the 204 // seats outside the 4 originally-sourced blocks aren't publicly compiled // here, so the margin (not the winner, which is real) is estimated from // each block's aggregate vote shares. // Real, sourced full vote-share breakdowns for individual districts outside // the 4 originally-sourced blocks, gathered from each district's own // Wikipedia page (not just the winner, which was already real for all 289 // — this is every party's actual share). Not exhaustive: of the 204 seats // outside the sourced blocks, only these have been individually verified // so far; the rest still fall back to the block-aggregate approximation // below. 32.4, sanseito: 11.3 }, "Nagasaki-3": { ldp: 72.1, cra: 27.9 }, "Niigata-5": { ldp: 52.1, cra: 39.6, sanseito: 8.2 }, "Shiga-1": { ldp: 42.0, ishin: 26.0, dpp: 21.4, jcp: 10.6 }, "Shiga-2": { ldp: 60.1, cra: 20.0, ishin: 19.9 }, "Hiroshima-1": { ldp: 53.3, cra: 18.2, sanseito: 17.0, reiwa: 4.6, jcp: 4.2, ind: 2.5 }, "Aomori-1": { ldp: 50.9, cra: 32.6, sanseito: 10.0, jcp: 6.5 }, }; function generateNotionalSeats(block) { const seats = []; const blockDistricts = DISTRICTS.filter((d) => d.block === block.id); const byParty = {}; blockDistricts.forEach((d) => { (byParty[d.winner] = byParty[d.winner] || []).push(d); }); Object.entries(byParty).forEach(([p, districts]) => { districts.sort((a, b) => a.num - b.num); // deterministic order const n = districts.length; // challenger = strongest OTHER party by the block's aggregate vote share let challenger = null, bestOther = -1; PARTY_IDS.forEach((q) => { if (q === p) return; const v = block.smd[q] || 0; if (v > bestOther) { bestOther = v; challenger = q; } }); // Average margin ~ block's overall gap between p and its challenger; // floored at 3pts for a party that wins seats here despite not being // the block's overall top-2 (e.g. a local independent upset) — the // aggregate gap would otherwise be negative or meaningless for them. const marginAvg = Math.max((block.smd[p] || 0) - bestOther, 3); districts.forEach((district, idx) => { const i = idx + 1; const real = REAL_DISTRICT_SHARES[`${district.pref}-${district.num}`]; let baseShares, actualChallenger; if (real) { // Real, sourced per-party shares for this specific seat — use // directly rather than the block-average approximation. baseShares = { ...block.smd, ...real }; actualChallenger = challenger; } else { // linear spread across n seats, from marginal to safe, averaging // to marginAvg: values (2i-1)/n for i=1..n average to exactly 1. const margin = marginAvg * ((2 * i - 1) / n); baseShares = { ...block.smd }; baseShares[p] = (block.smd[challenger] || 0) + margin; actualChallenger = challenger; } seats.push({ winner: p, challenger: actualChallenger, baseShares, kucode: district.kucode }); }); }); return seats; } BLOCKS.forEach((b) => (b.notionalSeats = generateNotionalSeats(b))); function resolveNotionalSeats(block, deltas, taperFactor, perDistrict, perDistrictShares) { const totals = {}; PARTY_IDS.forEach((p) => (totals[p] = 0)); block.notionalSeats.forEach((seat) => { const swung = {}; let sum = 0; PARTY_IDS.forEach((p) => { const v = Math.max(0, (seat.baseShares[p] || 0) + (deltas[p] || 0)) * taperFactor[p]; swung[p] = v; sum += v; }); let best = null, bestShare = -Infinity; PARTY_IDS.forEach((p) => { if (swung[p] > bestShare) { bestShare = swung[p]; best = p; } }); totals[best] += 1; if (perDistrict) perDistrict[seat.kucode] = best; if (perDistrictShares) { const normalized = {}; PARTY_IDS.forEach((p) => (normalized[p] = sum > 0 ? (swung[p] / sum) * 100 : 0)); perDistrictShares[seat.kucode] = normalized; } }); return totals; } function applySwing(basePct, deltas) { const swung = {}; PARTY_IDS.forEach((p) => (swung[p] = Math.max(0, (basePct[p] || 0) + (deltas[p] || 0)))); return normalizeTo100(swung); } // ============================================================ // Hemicycle layout — seats are placed on concentric arcs, but the radial // gap between arcs is set equal to the gap between seats within an arc // (both derive from the same "spacing" value), so the result reads as an // evenly-spaced field of dots rather than a few obviously distinct rings. // ============================================================ function computeHemicycleSlots(total) { const seatR = 5.6; const gap = 2.8; const spacing = seatR * 2 + gap; // shared radial + tangential spacing unit const maxR = 258; const buildRows = (minR) => { const rows = []; for (let r = minR; r <= maxR; r += spacing) { rows.push({ radius: r, capacity: Math.max(1, Math.floor((Math.PI * r) / spacing)) }); } return rows; }; // search for the innermost radius that fills the arc with exactly // `total` seats at this spacing (falls back to the closest overshoot) let best = null; for (let minR = 40; minR <= 160; minR += 1) { const rows = buildRows(minR); const sum = rows.reduce((a, r) => a + r.capacity, 0); if (sum >= total) { const diff = sum - total; if (!best || diff < best.diff) best = { rows, sum, diff }; if (diff === 0) break; } } const rows = best ? best.rows : buildRows(40); // trim any overshoot evenly off the outermost rows so the total is exact let overshoot = best ? best.diff : 0; for (let i = rows.length - 1; i >= 0 && overshoot > 0; i--) { const take = Math.min(overshoot, rows[i].capacity - 1); rows[i].capacity -= take; overshoot -= take; } const slots = []; rows.forEach(({ radius, capacity }) => { for (let s = 0; s < capacity; s++) { const angle = Math.PI - (Math.PI * (s + 0.5)) / capacity; slots.push({ angle, x: 300 + radius * Math.cos(angle), y: 298 - radius * Math.sin(angle) }); } }); slots.sort((a, b) => b.angle - a.angle); // left to right return slots; } const HEMICYCLE_SLOTS = computeHemicycleSlots(465); const HEMICYCLE_ORDER = ["ldp", "ishin", "genzei", "cpj", "dpp", "mirai", "cra", "sanseito", "sdp", "reiwa", "jcp", "euth", "indalliance", "yamato", "jlp", "rebirth", "firststar", "worldpeace", "futureprog", "nuclearfusion", "heart", "ind"]; const SLIDER_PARTY_IDS = PARTY_IDS.filter((p) => !MICRO_PARTY_IDS.includes(p) && p !== "ind"); // Each district's is its own memoized component keyed by kucode, so // a slider drag — which recomputes results.districtWinners on every frame — // only causes React to re-render the handful of districts whose winner // actually flipped, instead of reconciling all 289 on every tick. This // matters most for the largest parties (e.g. LDP, CRA), where a single // swing can flip winner status in dozens of districts simultaneously. const DistrictPath = React.memo(function DistrictPath({ district, fill, title }) { return ( {title} ); }); // Blends a party's real color from a light neutral base up to full // saturation as its district vote share rises, so a strength map reads as // "how strong is this party here" via color intensity rather than a single // winner-takes-all fill. maxScale sets the share (%) at which a district // renders at the party's true, undiluted color — safe seats can clear 80%, // so anything at or above that is already "fully" that party's color. function blendPartyStrength(hex, pct, maxScale = 75) { const base = { r: 0xE3, g: 0xDF, b: 0xD3 }; // panel background tone const h = hex.replace("#", ""); const target = { r: parseInt(h.substring(0, 2), 16), g: parseInt(h.substring(2, 4), 16), b: parseInt(h.substring(4, 6), 16), }; const t = Math.max(0, Math.min(1, pct / maxScale)); const r = Math.round(base.r + (target.r - base.r) * t); const g = Math.round(base.g + (target.g - base.g) * t); const b = Math.round(base.b + (target.b - base.b) * t); return `#${r.toString(16).padStart(2, "0")}${g.toString(16).padStart(2, "0")}${b.toString(16).padStart(2, "0")}`; } const STRENGTH_MAP_PARTY_IDS = [...SLIDER_PARTY_IDS, "ind"]; export default function JapanElectionCalculator() { const [prPct, setPrPct] = useState({ ...NATIONAL_PR_PCT }); const pendingUpdate = useRef(null); const rafId = useRef(null); // Sliders move independently, but an increase is clamped so the total // across all 11 never exceeds 100% — once the leftover hits 0%, nothing // else can go up (only down, which frees room for the others again). // // A native can fire onChange many times faster than // the screen can repaint during a fast drag; each one used to trigger a // full synchronous re-render of the 465-node hemicycle plus the results // table, which is what made dragging feel laggy. This coalesces however // many events land within one animation frame into a single state // update, capping re-renders at the display's own refresh rate instead // of the input device's event rate. const handleSlider = (partyId, newVal) => { newVal = Math.max(0, Math.min(100, newVal)); pendingUpdate.current = { partyId, newVal }; if (rafId.current == null) { rafId.current = requestAnimationFrame(() => { rafId.current = null; const { partyId, newVal } = pendingUpdate.current; setPrPct((prev) => { const otherSlidersSum = SLIDER_PARTY_IDS.filter((p) => p !== partyId).reduce((a, p) => a + prev[p], 0); const maxAllowed = Math.max(0, 100 - otherSlidersSum); return { ...prev, [partyId]: Math.min(newVal, maxAllowed) }; }); }); } }; const reset = () => setPrPct({ ...NATIONAL_PR_PCT }); const slidersSum = SLIDER_PARTY_IDS.reduce((a, p) => a + prPct[p], 0); const leftoverPct = Math.max(0, 100 - slidersSum); // sliders are clamped so this never goes negative // Distribute any CHANGE in the leftover budget across the 10 real, named // micro-parties and Independents in their real 2026 relative proportions // — additively, not as a multiplicative ratio. A ratio against the tiny // micro-party baseline (~0.14%) meant a modest single-slider move could // multiply it 20-30x, and that same multiplier then hit Independents' // much larger real baseline (2.08%), producing wildly oversized swings // from small inputs. Measuring the *change* in percentage points and // splitting that directly keeps every entity's movement proportionate // to how much the sliders actually moved. // // Memoized on prPct specifically (not rebuilt as a fresh object every // render) so the useMemo below it actually skips work on renders that // don't touch the sliders — e.g. toggling the methodology panel used to // silently re-run the full election model for no reason. const fullPct = useMemo(() => { const leftoverDelta = leftoverPct - MICRO_PARTY_PR_BASELINE_SUM; // 0 at true baseline const next = { ...prPct }; MICRO_PARTY_IDS.forEach((p) => { const share = LEFTOVER_GROUP_WEIGHT > 0 ? NATIONAL_PR_PCT[p] / LEFTOVER_GROUP_WEIGHT : 0; next[p] = Math.max(0, NATIONAL_PR_PCT[p] + leftoverDelta * share); }); // Independents have 0% real PR baseline (they can't file a list), so // their "list %" is just their proportional slice of the leftover // change, driving the delta mechanism below: 0 at true baseline, // growing or shrinking in step with however far the sliders moved. const indShare = LEFTOVER_GROUP_WEIGHT > 0 ? NATIONAL_SMD_PCT.ind / LEFTOVER_GROUP_WEIGHT : 0; next.ind = leftoverDelta * indShare; return next; }, [prPct, leftoverPct]); const results = useMemo(() => { const deltas = {}; PARTY_IDS.forEach((p) => (deltas[p] = fullPct[p] - NATIONAL_PR_PCT[p])); // Taper: a party's constituency vote share is tied to its list vote // share falling to zero too, not left floating on its own. Below 20% // of a party's real 2026 list result, its constituency share starts // scaling down proportionally, hitting zero exactly when the list // share does. Above that 20% threshold, constituency swing behaves // as normal (factor of 1). Freed-up share is reallocated to the // other parties in that constituency by renormalizing back to 100%. const taperFactor = {}; PARTY_IDS.forEach((p) => { const base = NATIONAL_PR_PCT[p]; const threshold = 0.2 * base; if (threshold <= 0) { taperFactor[p] = 1; // no meaningful list baseline to taper against (e.g. independents) return; } taperFactor[p] = fullPct[p] >= threshold ? 1 : Math.max(0, fullPct[p] / threshold); }); const totals = {}; PARTY_IDS.forEach((p) => (totals[p] = { pr: 0, smd: 0 })); const districtWinners = {}; const districtShares = {}; BLOCKS.forEach((block) => { const swungPr = applySwing(block.pr, deltas); const prAlloc = dHondt(swungPr, block.prSeats, block.prCaps); PARTY_IDS.forEach((p) => (totals[p].pr += prAlloc[p] || 0)); const smdAlloc = resolveNotionalSeats(block, deltas, taperFactor, districtWinners, districtShares); PARTY_IDS.forEach((p) => (totals[p].smd += smdAlloc[p] || 0)); }); // Displayed national constituency % is computed directly from the // sourced national baseline (not reconstructed by averaging the 11 // blocks) so it's exact at zero swing — averaging blocks drifted // slightly because the 7 modelled blocks' baseline was calibrated // using PR-seat weighting but was being aggregated here with // SMD-seat weighting, a mismatch that nudged the baseline off its // real, sourced value. const nationalSmdRaw = applySwing(NATIONAL_SMD_PCT, deltas); const taperedNationalRaw = {}; PARTY_IDS.forEach((p) => (taperedNationalRaw[p] = (nationalSmdRaw[p] || 0) * taperFactor[p])); const smdPctNational = normalizeTo100(taperedNationalRaw); return { totals, smdPctNational, districtWinners, districtShares }; }, [fullPct]); const totalSeatsByParty = useMemo(() => { const t = {}; PARTY_IDS.forEach((p) => (t[p] = results.totals[p].pr + results.totals[p].smd)); return t; }, [results.totals]); const hemicycleFill = useMemo(() => { const fill = []; HEMICYCLE_ORDER.forEach((p) => { for (let i = 0; i < totalSeatsByParty[p]; i++) fill.push(p); }); while (fill.length < 465) fill.push("__unfilled"); // rounding-gap filler; renders neutral gray, not a real party return fill.slice(0, 465); }, [totalSeatsByParty]); const [coalitionSelected, setCoalitionSelected] = useState(new Set()); const toggleCoalitionParty = (id) => { setCoalitionSelected((prev) => { const next = new Set(prev); if (next.has(id)) next.delete(id); else next.add(id); return next; }); }; // ---- Map pan/zoom ---- // MAP_BASE describes the default (fully zoomed-out) view; mapView tracks // the current center point (in the same coordinate space as the map's // path data) and zoom scale. The rendered viewBox is derived from these // on every render, so zoom/pan never touches the underlying path data. const MAP_BASE = { x: 15, y: 209, w: 423, h: 482, cx: 15 + 423 / 2, cy: 209 + 482 / 2 }; const [mapView, setMapView] = useState({ scale: 1, cx: MAP_BASE.cx, cy: MAP_BASE.cy }); const mapSvgRef = useRef(null); const mapDrag = useRef(null); const MIN_ZOOM = 1, MAX_ZOOM = 10; const mapViewBox = useMemo(() => { const w = MAP_BASE.w / mapView.scale; const h = MAP_BASE.h / mapView.scale; return { x: mapView.cx - w / 2, y: mapView.cy - h / 2, w, h }; }, [mapView]); const zoomMapAt = (factor, pxRatioX, pxRatioY) => { setMapView((prev) => { const w = MAP_BASE.w / prev.scale, h = MAP_BASE.h / prev.scale; const vbX = prev.cx - w / 2, vbY = prev.cy - h / 2; const ux = vbX + pxRatioX * w, uy = vbY + pxRatioY * h; const newScale = Math.min(MAX_ZOOM, Math.max(MIN_ZOOM, prev.scale * factor)); const newW = MAP_BASE.w / newScale, newH = MAP_BASE.h / newScale; const newVbX = ux - pxRatioX * newW, newVbY = uy - pxRatioY * newH; return { scale: newScale, cx: newVbX + newW / 2, cy: newVbY + newH / 2 }; }); }; const wheelPending = useRef(null); const wheelRaf = useRef(null); useEffect(() => { const el = mapSvgRef.current; if (!el) return; const handleWheel = (e) => { e.preventDefault(); const rect = el.getBoundingClientRect(); const pxRatioX = (e.clientX - rect.left) / rect.width; const pxRatioY = (e.clientY - rect.top) / rect.height; const factor = e.deltaY < 0 ? 1.2 : 1 / 1.2; // Coalesce however many wheel events land within one animation frame // into a single state update — a fast trackpad/mouse can fire dozens // of wheel events per second, and without this, each one triggered a // full synchronous re-render of a very heavy tree (289 map paths + // 465 hemicycle circles + tables), which could hang or crash the tab. wheelPending.current = { factor: (wheelPending.current ? wheelPending.current.factor : 1) * factor, pxRatioX, pxRatioY, }; if (wheelRaf.current == null) { wheelRaf.current = requestAnimationFrame(() => { wheelRaf.current = null; const pending = wheelPending.current; wheelPending.current = null; if (pending) zoomMapAt(pending.factor, pending.pxRatioX, pending.pxRatioY); }); } }; el.addEventListener("wheel", handleWheel, { passive: false }); return () => { el.removeEventListener("wheel", handleWheel); if (wheelRaf.current != null) cancelAnimationFrame(wheelRaf.current); }; }, []); const dragRaf = useRef(null); const dragPending = useRef(null); const handleMapPointerDown = (e) => { mapDrag.current = { startX: e.clientX, startY: e.clientY, startCx: mapView.cx, startCy: mapView.cy, moved: false }; e.currentTarget.setPointerCapture(e.pointerId); }; const handleMapPointerMove = (e) => { if (!mapDrag.current || !mapSvgRef.current) return; // Same coalescing as the wheel handler: pointermove can fire far more // often than the screen repaints, so only the latest position within // each animation frame actually triggers a state update. dragPending.current = { clientX: e.clientX, clientY: e.clientY }; if (dragRaf.current == null) { dragRaf.current = requestAnimationFrame(() => { dragRaf.current = null; const pending = dragPending.current; dragPending.current = null; const drag = mapDrag.current; // snapshot now — see note below if (!pending || !drag || !mapSvgRef.current) return; const rect = mapSvgRef.current.getBoundingClientRect(); const w = MAP_BASE.w / mapView.scale, h = MAP_BASE.h / mapView.scale; const dxPix = pending.clientX - drag.startX; const dyPix = pending.clientY - drag.startY; if (Math.abs(dxPix) > 2 || Math.abs(dyPix) > 2) drag.moved = true; const dxUser = (dxPix / rect.width) * w; const dyUser = (dyPix / rect.height) * h; // Capture the plain numbers here rather than referencing mapDrag.current // inside the updater below — setMapView's functional updater can run // slightly after this point (e.g. if a pointerup fires and nulls the // ref in the gap before React invokes it), which was crashing with // "Cannot read properties of null" even though the guard above passed. const startCx = drag.startCx, startCy = drag.startCy; setMapView((prev) => ({ ...prev, cx: startCx - dxUser, cy: startCy - dyUser })); }); } }; const [selectedDistrict, setSelectedDistrict] = useState(null); const [strengthParty, setStrengthParty] = useState(null); // null = normal winner map const popupRef = useRef(null); useEffect(() => { if (selectedDistrict == null) return; const handleOutsideClick = (e) => { if (popupRef.current && !popupRef.current.contains(e.target)) { setSelectedDistrict(null); } }; // pointerdown (not click) so this fires before the map's own // pointerup-based district-selection logic — clicking a different // district still closes this popup and opens the new one cleanly, // rather than fighting over which handler runs first. document.addEventListener("pointerdown", handleOutsideClick); return () => document.removeEventListener("pointerdown", handleOutsideClick); }, [selectedDistrict]); const [popupPos, setPopupPos] = useState({ x: 0, y: 0 }); const handleMapPointerUp = (e) => { // A tap that didn't turn into a drag selects whatever district is // actually under the cursor. elementFromPoint (rather than e.target) // is used deliberately — while this element has active pointer // capture during a drag, event targeting can get redirected to the // capturing element itself, which would make e.target unreliable here. const wasDrag = !!(mapDrag.current && mapDrag.current.moved); mapDrag.current = null; dragPending.current = null; if (dragRaf.current != null) { cancelAnimationFrame(dragRaf.current); dragRaf.current = null; } if (e && e.currentTarget && e.pointerId != null && e.currentTarget.hasPointerCapture && e.currentTarget.hasPointerCapture(e.pointerId)) { e.currentTarget.releasePointerCapture(e.pointerId); if (kucode && mapSvgRef.current) { const rect = mapSvgRef.current.getBoundingClientRect(); setPopupPos({ x: e.clientX - rect.left, y: e.clientY - rect.top }); setSelectedDistrict(Number(kucode)); } } }; const resetMapView = () => setMapView({ scale: 1, cx: MAP_BASE.cx, cy: MAP_BASE.cy }); const mapIsZoomed = mapView.scale > 1.02; const mainMapPaths = useMemo(() => { if (strengthParty) { const party = PARTIES.find((p) => p.id === strengthParty); return ; }); } return DISTRICTS.map((d) => { const winner = results.districtWinners[d.kucode] || d.winner; const party = PARTIES.find((p) => p.id === winner); const fill = party ? party.color : "#B5B0A3"; const title = `${d.pref} ${d.num} — ${party ? party.name : "Independent"}`; return ; }); }, [results.districtWinners, results.districtShares, strengthParty]); const darkenColor = (hex, amount = 0.35) => { const h = hex.replace("#", ""); const r = parseInt(h.substring(0, 2), 16); const g = parseInt(h.substring(2, 4), 16); const b = parseInt(h.substring(4, 6), 16); const dr = Math.round(r * (1 - amount)); const dg = Math.round(g * (1 - amount)); const db = Math.round(b * (1 - amount)); return `#${dr.toString(16).padStart(2, "0")}${dg.toString(16).padStart(2, "0")}${db.toString(16).padStart(2, "0")}`; }; const selectedDistrictInfo = useMemo(() => { if (selectedDistrict == null) return null;
